My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00190/index.shtml -- 190/M]] Will you use your ministerial colleagues to ensure that the Federal Minister for Foreign Affairs in the Council of Ministers of the EU exercises its veto on the issue of Easter enlargement if the acceding States do not previously shut down their nuclear power plants?
ParlaMint-AT_1998-01-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00107_d7e3019	Please, Mr. Minister.

I should like to remind you that last summer, an all-party resolution request was made to all parties represented in Parliament, including yours, which redefines Austria's anti-atomic policy, which clearly states that Austria continues to pursue the principle of a nuclear-free central Europe. This is a medium- and long-term objective, which we are committed to, and which, of course, we will continue during the accession negotiations for our Central and Eastern European neighbours, which will take place in the coming years. This includes helping, at a very early stage, to determine energy efficiency potential in these countries, to define transition scenarios, to define exit scenarios and to clarify them – here we are not alone, even at the EU Council in Luxembourg last December – that nuclear safety plays a major role for the European Union as a whole, but of course also in the coming accessions of some Central and Eastern European countries, especially some neighbouring countries of Austria. This is clear, and compliance with Western security principles must be ensured. I have made that quite clear several times. That's our strategy. As I said, Objective number one: a nuclear-free Central Europe, and hence the exit from nuclear power in the long term, which, of course, also applies to the candidate countries. Objective 2 : that, at least as a minimum, Western security principles are respected. We can and will ask the candidate countries to do so, which will be an essential part of Austria's policy in the course of the accession negotiations.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-01-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00107_d7e4081	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-01-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00107_d7e4097	Not at all, Mr Schuster, ladies and gentlemen, because Austria's family policy and also the knowledge of the Constitutional Court assume that there is a fair balance between those who remain childless and those who have children who have obligations in the course of education, who must provide maintenance services which also cost money. The Liberal Forum's model, on the other hand, assumes that it will not be possible for family support until families can no longer be helped by their own means, so that they become socially weak, so that the state can help. This is a completely different approach, which I do not support and which cannot be reconciled with Austria's traditional family policy.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-01-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00107_d7e4236	Please, Mr. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-01-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00107_d7e4251	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to thank you very much for the excellent work you have done in At the Kyoto Climate Change Conference, the world's industrialized countries have committed themselves to reducing their greenhouse gas emissions by an average of 5.2 percent by 2010; the European Union by 8 percent, the Americans by 7 percent, the Japanese by 6 percent. This results in a reduction of 5.2 percent for the industrialized countries as a whole. From the European Union's point of view, it is now possible to redefine the burden distribution within the Union. The European Union has gone to Kyoto with a minus 15 percent reduction target, which was the mandate. The outcome of Kyoto is now to be discussed. It is important to examine which EU countries are to take on what burdens. I call for Austria to rethink its commitment within the European Union. At the same time, however, I advocate that we maintain the so-called Toronto target as a national commitment, i.e. that we try to reduce our CO2 emissions – carbon dioxide is the most important greenhouse gas by 20 percent by 2005.

[[Congratulations on the liberals.]] If we look at how taxes increased compared with 1995 we only need to refer to the accounts. Other revenues or privatisations have been added to the total of 4 billion compared to 1995. That is, more than 60 billion schillings have come in. If the need for consolidation for 1996 is only 60 billion, then it can be found that this need for consolidation has been met only on the basis of tax increases. We also criticised the Minister of Finance at the time that the implementation or the deficit in the form he presented probably does not correspond to the facts. At the time, we listed to him examples, for example, that the Federal Government has held invoices that it has not carried out transfers for payment commitments which would have been due in the months of the third quarter of the year, but that these payments have only been made in January of the following year. It was all put down as nonsense. If I look through the success of the conduct – and I take the basis of the Court of Auditors' report again – I note that the outstanding commitments increased from 8 billion in 1996 to more than 20 billion. This is an increase of almost 12 billion schilling. There can therefore be no mention of a proper budget implementation! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] This budget deficit, which is 89 billion schilling, does not correspond to the facts, but should be added at least to those 12 billion, so that a deficit of over 100 billion already results. It is therefore also a question of the fact that this fiscal policy has also led to a result in the EU as a whole, namely that those countries which have a high tax and tax rate are lagging behind in economic growth and that the countries with a low tax and tax rate have a high economic growth, such as the Netherlands. And another Mar with which you should really clean up: They have repeatedly said that tax reductions lead to less revenue. And now, when I come back to the Court of Auditors' report, I notice that a decided one stands inside – I only show it by means of a small example –: In the case of sparkling wine tax, there has been more revenue in the order of 68 million schilling. This is due to an disproportionate increase in turnover due to tax reduction. This is another indication of the fact that tax reduction does not lead to tax reductions, but rather leads to increased economic growth, more jobs and thus indirect higher tax revenue, because there are fewer unemployed people, the economy is growing, and the demand for consumer goods and also for investment goods is higher, which should also give rise to a pleasure for entrepreneurs. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] And that's why: Don't just talk about making a tax reform in 2000! Take the paper of the Freemans at hand! You are enslaving around now by increasing or introducing other taxes. Do you implement this programme in the sense of Austria in order to achieve a healthy economic growth and jobs for the population?

The budget deficit, which was still 117.9 billion schilling in 1995, was reduced by 24.2 percent to 89.4 billion schilling. They were recorded as tax revenues, although they are de facto de facto a non-profit credit of the taxpayers to the State and they are purely economically not tax revenue in the usual sense. As a result – and this has always been the subject of the debate between Minister Edlinger and me – I have claimed that these 15 billion schillings are not deficit-effective, i.e. the real deficit of 1998 is actually 15 billion shillings higher than official, or 0.6 percent of GDP higher. This follows – I said at the time – from the philosophy of the Maastricht demarcation, which one can now argue about for a long time; but the Maastricht demarcation of a deficit now follows the so-called "acrual principle" and not the cash principle, while the Ministry of Finance acts as if the cash principle were the normal and the usual. Mr Edlinger's argument was always the following: That may be already, but that is Maastricht-compliant, because all the other countries – I think, except Italy – do so. I have taken note of this argument, even though it has been more and more committed to political rationality than the subject matter logic: Only because they all do, it must not yet be Maastricht-compliant. However, I have always said the following: I admit that EUROSTAT has often decided incomprehensiblely, but that the Maastricht agreement should be, I will not believe until I have written it by EUROSTAT. In the meantime, I received it in writing from EUROSTAT. I very much recommend, Secretary of State, the "Eurostat News Release" of 17 May. ==References====External links== This is a question of how the central bank is to be dealt with in budgetary terms and with due dates for taxes and how they affect the deficit. In my opinion, it is clear that in the case of taxes on production and imports, i.e. primarily on VAT, it is imperative to book at the time of the transaction's creation. You don't have any room to play at 13. In the case of transfers or other questions relating to the effectiveness of the tax. It comes only to the time of the transaction. In so far as the tax assets are VAT - and I assume that most of these funds are VAT revenue - these 15 billion schillings, which you book as revenue, are not Maastricht-compliant. Similarly – although with another reason – it deals with income taxes and the rest of wealth taxes – in Austria it is mainly income taxes. One cannot simply see from a comparison of the two accounts in 1995 and 1996 where this improvement has been achieved. We are in the process of progressing the administrative reform efforts which are most likely to reduce the structural deficit at the end of the day. We are in the process of going through resorts to see if there are any tasks that the state itself should no longer carry out today, which are better dealt with by the market. We are in the process of examining the location for the resort, whether there are areas where we can carry out the task better or closer to the citizen. And we are also in the process of applying the most modern instruments in the administration, because this saving cannot, of course, be carried out on the backs of public servants, but we must try, through the most modern instruments, to stop work compression and to try to become more efficient in this area too. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-01-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00107_d7e7228	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! High house! The Federal Government has set itself the top objective of fiscal consolidation for the year 1996, and it is therefore obvious that the Federal Accounting Office, which is a reflection of the budget implementation this year, is concerned with this and that the Court of Auditors makes a number of observations in the federal accounts. It has already been done a lot by my previous speakers to see whether this objective has now been achieved and how it has been achieved. If one reads the federal accounts, if one looks at the data and compares the objectives set by the Federal Government, one will see that there is no particular reason for the Court of Auditors itself for euphoria or for resignation. For the Court of Auditors, it should be noted that what happened in 1996 in the budget, but also in the budget implementation, is a fundamental rethinking, a departure from the thinking of the years before, and the objective of achieving the Maastricht criteria. If the Maastricht criteria are now examined in detail, it will be noted that the public deficit could be reduced in 1996 compared to the previous years, but that the 3% shown by Maastricht has not yet been reached. Although the figures for the year 1997 are not yet final, it can be expected that 1997 will show even better figures. In any case, the 3 percent have not yet been reached in 1996, but it is a non-insignificant reduction in the public deficit to register positively, as the Court of Auditors believes. The reasons which are important for this reduction are multiple. The Court of Auditors does not fall into a black and white painting, but endeavours, and has expressed this in the federal accounts, to mention all the possibilities it has raised. There is no doubt that an increase in revenue has been achieved by increasing the tax rate. This was also enshrined in the objectives of the Federal Government. Whether the ratio could actually be reached one third on the revenue side, two thirds on the expenditure side, may at the moment remain. The Court of Auditors has referred to sources which dispute this relationship. He himself will examine it even further during this year. As a second reason for achieving the Maastricht criteria or achieving an approximation to these criteria, there is no doubt that the good results of the countries and municipalities have been achieved. For the Maastricht criterion of the public deficit is not only the debt of the federal government, it is not only the deficit of the federal government, but also the deficits of all local authorities. There is no doubt that the good results of the countries and communities have had a positive effect. In so far as this positive development should not be written exclusively on the account of the federal government, for there are undoubtedly other influences to a greater extent. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] A third reason why the Maastricht criterion could be approximated is the savings that could be made, savings which, however, the Court of Auditors concludes, are essentially linear and not due to a deep criticism of tasks. To this end, the Court of Auditors believes that this task critique should not be missing in the future and should not be missing. Only with a truly profound task critique, across the administration as a whole, will it be possible to achieve lasting success, both in terms of budget consolidation and in terms of achieving the Maastricht criteria. A structural reform in public administration therefore seems essential to me; this cannot be repeated often enough. The Court of Auditors has already done this in recent years, and it is positive that in 1996, at least one has been shown to take seriously its reminders over the past few years. In the Court's view, however, far too little has been done. As a structural reform has been mentioned in the public administration, I can say that there is no doubt that a mere or comprehensive abolition of professional civil service in public administration will not be possible. I even consider this to be something subordinate in relation to budget consolidation and structural reform measures. Surely you won't be able to find out. Finally, in order to improve the Maastricht "public deficit" criterion, it was also important that accountancy measures were applied, as was explained in the Court of Auditors' accounts. I would like to stress that these are legal settlement measures, but at the same time I would like to add that these conversion measures cannot save a profound structural reform. There should be no illusion of achieving fiscal consolidation through permanent such measures and thus delaying structural reform. Public debt represents the second fiscal Maastricht criterion. In this respect too, the Federal Government's aim was to improve through the 1996 budget. This has not occurred. In 1996, public debt rose by about 70 percent. If the figures for 1997 show an improvement in this respect, the Court of Auditors will: However, it will also be subject to a review before it is satisfied. I would like to draw attention to what public debt is all about, but also to a warning. More emphasis has been placed in the recent past on achieving the public deficit – the famous 3 percent – and perhaps less on achieving the Maastricht criterion of the public debt level of 60 percent. I should therefore like to warn, because the public debt situation is ultimately also important to the extent that the cost of the interest on the financial debt is high, because, as the Court of Auditors has also stated in the federal accounts, the interest rate on financial debt has already reached a higher level than economic growth in the last year. So we should continue to work to reduce the overall level of public debt, which has not yet been achieved in 1996, which is under discussion today. In addition, it must not be overlooked that the burden of the federal government, the federal budget in an indirect form, is also borne by liabilities and out-of-budget financing. In this respect, the Maastricht criteria do not provide an anchor in order to be able to make a reliable statement that, by reaching these Maastricht criteria alone, an improvement would have already been achieved in other ways, in view of the liability and out-of-budget financing. The Court of Auditors has repeatedly urged this in the past. He will do so in the future. There is no doubt that the monetary Maastricht criteria have been achieved, not only in 1996, but before, and it has never been discussed that these criteria could be missed. So if one wants a short summary, it is also positive - from the Court of Auditors - that there has been a rethinking that, as I think, one has taken serious steps to consolidate the budget, but that no structural budget adjustment and no structural reform of the public administration have yet taken place, which will no doubt be remedied. A number of important steps were therefore taken in 1996 which are welcomed by the Court of Auditors. However, there are still many steps to be taken in respect of which both the Federal Government and the National Council must be aware that there is still a great deal of effort to achieve the stated objective. The Court of Auditors will continue to have an eye on this objective to ensure that the efforts made by the administration, by the government, by the National Council also contribute to the implementation of budgetary consolidation as we all want it to be. However, I would like to remind you that it is not only a question of achieving a specific objective that has not yet been achieved – and I would like to stress this once again – but rather that – and this also requires the Maastricht Treaty – it must be a sustainable budget consolidation. And to this extent we are called upon to contribute ours so that this can be achieved. As a general rule, therefore, the 1996 accounts for the Federal Republic of Germany represent an interim marking on a path which we believe is in the right direction, but whose objective is still a long way off. To achieve this will still require great effort.

They criticise the fact that Appendix 1, Appendix 2, Part I and Part III of the Protocol, as it were, are raised into the government's responsibilities through constitutional change and are no longer submitted to the National Council. It now raises the question of what is contained in these parts – all other parts of the agreement, if they are amended, of course, in the National Council. These parts include a list of seismological stations. That is to say, if this list is to be amended, we would have to decide by law in the National Council if we were to submit your wish. That's what I'm looking at, how to make fun of it when I'm asked: Why does the Austrian National Council have to decide that Novosibirsk will be made a seismological station instead of Irkutsk? I even think that the reporting obligation which we have imposed on the Foreign Minister for precisely these technical issues is an exaggeration; but, well, it should be that way. As far as the formal. [[Call to the liberals: Better, he must report!]] Okay, better, he tells us about Novosibirsk than not at all. But, I fear, this is a bit of a thing in Mr Spindelegger's contribution, but I hope that I am wrong - do not reject us now. This step is one of a total of six steps proposed by the Canberra Commission – a Commission of the Australian Government with international experts – to subject nuclear weapons to disarmament as a whole. For it is not about – this danger is very great and increases daily – that the world is being contaminated atomicly, not because it comes to a military struggle, but from pure slutting. [[Complementary of the Abg. Dr. Krammer.]] The nuclear submarines are currently rotting in front of themselves. However, it is essential to take another step, and this is the problem. They are also stationed in various parts of the world – on which we should not give in to illusion. For what? – This would be the first step the Canberra Commission recommends: to disable these sharpened weapons, so that an nuclear war is not triggered by an accident. I would like to draw particular attention to the second step, because it is said again and again: What good is it when people collect signatures and get involved? That won't help! – Last year, we experienced that the Greenham Common nuclear missile base – where women in particular protested for years, which they besieged, where they were arrested or turned away, around which one had pulled a barbed wire wall after another – has become what he originally was, namely, a park. Now there are no more nuclear missiles to be paraded on trucks, but there are children playing there again and going for a walk. This shows what each of us can do, what the resistance of each of us and what every protest can do. That is why I would like us to take the nuclear testing agreement which is being debated today to make it our task to work towards the international implementation of the next five steps of the Canberra Commission and not to wait for an accident to happen, which may no longer be stopped. A word about the Consular Fee Act, the second matter of law to be dealt with: This is an adjustment as a result of our membership of the Schengen Agreement. I think that we, especially our European Parliamentarians, should take up a task that corresponds to good Austrian practice of bilateral agreements, especially to small countries visited by Austrian tourists and in which Austrian scientists work. I am thinking of the state of Saint Lucia with its 200,000 inhabitants, in which Austrian excavations are carried out and which is a destination of many tourists. [[Abg. Dr Gredler: A beautiful island!]] Small countries like this should be excluded from the visa requirement, including within the European framework. Because even if all 200,000 Saint Lucians make their way to Europe, that will be feasible. [[Abg. Dr Gredler: That's very nice!]] I think we need to pass this on to our European Parliamentarians. ParlaMint-AT_1998-01-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00107_d7e8197	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! The subject matter to be dealt with today is a global ban on all nuclear testing and nuclear explosions for peaceful purposes. This issue is therefore very important in terms of security policy, because "no tests" of course also mean: no further development of nuclear weapons. Those tests, which will still be allowed under this contract, will only be used in each case to verify the operational capacity of the existing weapons in the laboratory. At the same time, this Treaty also has environmental importance because it will certainly achieve a lower radiation exposure worldwide. Furthermore, minimization of the ecological risks arising from such nuclear tests can be achieved. Members of the Spindelegger have already said this: It was a very great success of Austria that its candidate for the seat of the CTBTO in Vienna was successful. This was not least a great success of the Austrian Foreign Minister and Deputy Chancellor, the Secretary of State and the officials of the House, whom I would like to thank very much for this. This seat in Vienna means that an important new international organization will be established in Vienna, an international organization which already employs about 100 people and will grow to 190 employees by the end of 1998. In 1998 alone, around 150 meetings and around 450 expert meetings will take place in Vienna, to which participants from all countries of the world will arrive and will therefore also be guests of this country. Of course, you will also experience tourism in the conference city of Vienna. Another advantage is the active participation of Austria in the construction of this organisation, and this is also important in terms of how the Treaty under negotiation will develop. A rapid ratification of this Treaty by Austria appears to be very important for five reasons: firstly, as a political signal to achieve as quickly as possible a large number of ratifications by the first review conference in 1999, second, to produce a political pressure within the EU on non-signatory countries, thirdly because of the political signal effect of the ratification of the seat state – eight ratifications are already in place, including Japan and the Czech Republic, so that it is important that Austria ratifys very quickly – fourthly to create a favourable climate for further nuclear disarmament steps through a speedy ratification process and fifthly and lastly for the purpose of confirming the strict rejection of nuclear tests by Austria, which indeed enjoys full retention among the Austrian population. Finally, this Treaty means the consistent continuation of the clear Austrian anti-atomic policy. Mrs Moser has also pointed out that the threats are not only caused by nuclear tests, but also by unsafe nuclear power plants in the border area. It is therefore particularly important to address this area in the context of EU enlargement. It is thanks to Foreign Minister Schüssel for the success of including the safety of nuclear power plants as a criterion in the position paper on the enlargement of the European Union. Ladies and gentlemen! We all know that it is unlikely that nuclear power plants in the border area in Central and Eastern Europe will be able to achieve a sufficient safety standard for the EU, which would mean that the closure of such nuclear power plants could also be achieved in the medium term. In any case, it is very important to continue Austria's struggle for a nuclear-free Central Europe and a nuclear-free world.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-01-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00107_d7e8245	The next speaker is Mr Magistrate. Posch. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-01-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00107_d7e8266	Minister! Mr President. High house! The present comprehensive nuclear test stop contract progressed for decades. Hundreds of thousands of people have been killed not only by nuclear bombs and reactor accidents, but also by nuclear tests, whether on Mururoa, Kazakhstan or Nevada. The long-term effects due to the enormous half-life – for example at plutonium for 24 000 years – are not yet estimated. And it was not x-any small states that organized this, but it was the great powers. It is all the more surprising that the international community has taken 50 years to stop nuclear tests. Paradoxically, it was in 1954 India – the state that now refuses to ratify – which made a first proposal for an agreement on a nuclear test ban. In 1958 a first nuclear testing conference was opened by the United States, the Soviet Union and the United Kingdom in Geneva, with the United States and the USSR in addition to a moratorium, and also with a view to negotiating direct inspections of test sites, monitoring committees and other control measures. The next attempt to restrict nuclear testing in July 1963 led to an agreement between the USA and the USSR on a partial ban on testing in the atmosphere, under water and space. As a result, the United States, the Soviet Union and Great Britain finally came to the end of all tests in the atmosphere. In 1974 this agreement was supplemented by an agreement on underground nuclear weapons attempts with an explosive force of over 150 kilotonnes of TNT. However, only thanks to a resolution unanimously adopted by the United Nations Assembly did the Disarmament Conference in Geneva in 1993 receive a strong mandate to negotiate a general test stop agreement, which, however, has not prevented China and France from continuing its nuclear weapons attempts. It was not until 1995 that the United Nations, with a unanimous resolution, strongly supported the conclusion of the negotiations at the Geneva Disarmament Conference in such a timely manner that this Test Stop Agreement of the 51. A meeting of the UN General Assembly was held. This has now been signed by more than 100 States and ratified by some States, so that the main obligations of the Treaty will soon come to an end. The Agreement obliges the members of the Treaty to discontinue all nuclear tests, and it also obliges them to undergo the facilities and all occurrences of international control, to allow their own inspectors and to establish an international verification system based on seismic, radio-nuclear, hydroacoustic and infrared surveillance. It is gratifying, as has been said, that the seat of this control organisation will be Austria and Vienna, where synergies can be used with the IAEA. It is therefore to be hoped that, as far as possible, all States of the Community will immediately ratify the Treaty in order to stop the nonsense of nuclear testing once and for all. In view of the importance of this work of the treaty and in the interests of efficient handling of it, I consider the collection of Article VII in the constitutional rank to be a margin. Thank you.

It is gratifying that the number of crimes committed by economic crime in connection with the transfer of counterfeit money has fallen substantially, as have the number of offences in the areas of physical injury and negligent physical injury and the number of crimes committed and the number of offences committed in the field of property damage. On the other hand, I believe that social policy has also contributed to the fact that, with regard to certain offences, certain transactions that take place in the circle of the family, a certain taboo has been broken, that one can speak openly about them and also bring the crime to light. This is positive and should remain the same. It is also fair to say here today that this safety report also shows that we are living in a safe country. First: Currently there are just 16 additional prisons for pushers in Bludenz in completion. These 16 additional detentions will be handed over in March of this year. Secondly, We are planning to build 66 additional thrusts in the police prisoner's house in Salzburg. We already agree with the country of Salzburg on the financing of de facto. Even in the first half of 1998, the construction of these 66 drawers will begin. Thirdly, Also in this year, up to 90 additional thrusts will be built in various building stages in the police prison in Vienna, so that there will be a clear discharge. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, in Burgenland, we are now doing more precisely: in Eisenstadt, to build 30 additional thrust sites, which there is very much... [[Abg. Kiss: The head of state speaks of 60!]] In the first stage there are 30 places; in another stage we want to build another 30. The 30 seats planned for the first stage will be completed in the first half of 1998. I expect you to support us in the establishment of resuscitation sites, Madam President. As you probably know, there is a citizens' initiative by the Freedom Party in Eisenstadt against the establishment of these sites. I do not think it is right if, on the one hand, it is criticized that there are too few places to push, and on the other hand, if one wants to build such, a citizens' initiative is launched by the same political movement. This means, in summary, that: We do everything we can to make additional push places available. I assume that by the end of the year we will have created 150 to 160 additional places and in 1999 we will create the remaining ones. We will also have to set up a small thruster in the area of the airport Schwechat, not quite as large as originally planned, but certainly in a way that allows the construction of 50 to 100 thrusters. This is to be done in a modular way, and I assume that we will succeed in achieving this objective by 1999. I would also like to point out something that is also particularly important: Infringements are not offenders, ladies and gentlemen, but offenders are people who are in breach of administration... [[Abg. Dr. Partik-Pablé: No, the perpetrators cannot be imprisoned because everything is already full!]] These are people who have committed a breach of administration. These are people who have entered our country illegally without permission. [[Abg. May. Stadler: This is also a crime! It's an administrative crime!]] That is why I do everything that is necessary so that we can send these people back to their countries as soon as possible. Therefore, the necessary international agreements will be concluded with the neighbouring countries concerned as soon as possible – in the coming months. I would also like, in order to make it quite clear to Mr Kiss, to assure the Vice-Chancellor Schüssel's initiative to combat tow crime all of my support. It is perfectly true that tow crime has become the most lucrative branch of international crime, organized crime. With extremely low effort and little risk, many people make a lot of money in the need and misery. This money – millions, if not billions of schilling – is being reused in further crime – whether the drug trade, forced prostitution, vehicle sliding or much more. I think that this initiative is particularly important so that people's smuggling is despised in every state of the world. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] We must work together to ensure that the UN creates appropriate criminal provisions in the countries concerned. We have already made the penalties for tow and I hope that in the coming processes the tractors will receive the appropriate penalties. Last year, more than 1,500 tugs were arrested in Austria. I hope that appropriate sanctions will also achieve a deterrent effect. The question of the revision of the Waffengesetz has already been discussed and taken up in detail. I would like to point out that, from my point of view, three things are necessary; and it is necessary that we have the courage to take the appropriate steps. Firstly, I believe that the acquisition of weapons for private property should be made much more difficult in the future. I assume that the security situation of the Republic of Austria is not in relation to the high number of weapons that exist in private households. A study shows that Austria has the sixth highest share of private arms in all the world's countries. But a private weapon does not mean higher security, but the opposite is the case. [[Abg. May. Stadler: That is why we are such a safe country!]] On the contrary: All experience shows that a weapon in the private budget is more insecure. There is much better means of protecting oneself. I would like to draw attention to the criminal police advisory service for each Member, which provides assistance. [[Abg. Young: If you have to wait 20 minutes in Vienna for a radio strip, Mr. Minister, how do you do that?]] [[Abg. Shoot yourself? Yeah?]] [[Abg. May. Stadler: Blecha ask!]] I'd like to tell you: Any safeguarding of the apartment and the house with an alarm system or with appropriate door locks, a dog or, for example, private pepper sprays – these are made possible by the new arms law – bring much more security than a weapon. [[Abg. Dr. Partik-Pablé: This is unrealistic!]] Secondly, I believe it is important for the Austrian executive to be able to control the way in which the weapon is kept in the private budget. Thirdly, I believe that the psychological tests should also be extended to all the holders of arms certificates who have acquired the weapon number before, and that it should be made clear that psychological tests cannot be carried out and repeated at all times. [[Abg. Dr. Partik-Pablé: Then you have to make sure that police can run outside when you say that everyone has to take a dog!]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! With regard to Schengen, I would like to say to Mrs Stoisits that a report on Austria's Presidency in the Schengen Executive Committee is currently being prepared by my ministry. I assume that this balance sheet will be delivered to all members of the Committee on the Interior in the next few days. I should also like to point out that before each meeting of the Schengen Executive Committee, in which Austria had the chair, I informed the Members of the Committee on the Interior of the content of the agenda and the corresponding initiatives of Austria. I think that we have done more than enough to meet the information needs and can assure you that I will continue this information activity over the next few months. I come to the last point, on the question of Kurd refugees who were stranded in southern Italy at the beginning of this year. Ladies and gentlemen! First, it would be wrong to reduce this problem exclusively to Kurdish refugees. According to my colleague from Italy, not only Kurds have fled but also a number of people from other nationalities and other countries have fled. Part of these people – I do not want to assess how much of this part is – have not fled due to political persecution, but they have been economic refugees, or – this is probably a better expression – poverty refugees who wanted to improve their personal economic and social situation. [[Abg. Dr. Partik-Pablé: That's what we always say! It's often adventurers!]] Austria has not closed its borders. Austria has asked for the following, Madam Member: Italy has given all the people who have fled to southern Italy the possibility of a proper asylum procedure. [[Abg. Dr. Partik-Pablé: Why don't you confess to being a poet?]] It can't be that a country says: We do not take the refugees in, but we issue a deportation notice and say that they have to leave the country within 14 days. If this happens, it is clear that these people are moving on: to Central Europe and to Western Europe and thus to Austria. It has therefore been necessary and important that Italy has been clearly called on by the Austrian side to ensure that everyone who has landed there also receives the possibility of a proper asylum procedure. Our pressure has contributed substantially to the fact that Italy has finally declared itself ready to do so. I think that was necessary, important and correct. In Italy, there were only 1,700 asylum procedures last year, ladies and gentlemen, whereas in Austria almost 7 000 asylum applications were made in the same year. [[Abg. Dr. Partik-Pablé: There you see where they go!]] This figure alone proves that very many people land in Italy and then move on. Our task must be to control the EU's external border very closely. If there is a risk of cross-border crime or illegal entry and immigration within the Member States of the European Union, it is up to the security bodies and the task of the Minister to take such action. This has always been agreed and is part of Schengen. Schengen means not only good control of the external borders against cross-border crime and against illegal entry, but also cooperation between the European Member States, so that it does not result in any less, but also in more security through border opening and travel freedom. Whenever this security is at risk and there is the possibility of illegal entry, it is the task of the Austrian security bodies to take action. [[Abg. Dkfm. Holger Bauer: Be careful, otherwise you will soon be in a far right country!]] That is why there will be controls at the German and Italian borders over the next few months and years in the form of veiling and in the form of appropriate compensation measures to ensure that security in our country is maintained at this level. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! In this sense, I am proud to present the 1996 Security Report with some pride together with my colleagues. I think that this safety report can be seen and clearly shows that Austria is one of the safest countries in Europe. Our common task should be to maintain this high safety standard in the next few years.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-01-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00107_d7e15604	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! First of all, a few basic words on the focus of justice policy on internal security. They result from the need for a determined fight against serious and organized crime on the one hand, but also from the need for the most meaningful perpetrator and victim-oriented reactions in the area of so-called everyday crime on the other. To the same extent, we need to ensure a rational execution, which is consistent with the safe custody of the criminal as well as with the optimal preparation for his release and reintegration into society. A number of legislative measures have been taken in recent years to improve the effectiveness of the fight against increasingly cross-border organised crime, both in the material and legal fields, in particular the new criminal offences, money laundering, criminal organisation, as well as the organized tow industry, as well as in the procedural field, in particular the use of enrichment and the special modern investigation methods already mentioned. In the area of organised crime, however, international cooperation is particularly important. That is why the Member States of the European Union have drawn up a comprehensive action plan to combat, but also to prevent organised crime, which was also adopted by the Amsterdam European Council. The implementation of this legislative and organisational action plan, partly the task of the Austrian Presidency in the second half of the year, will enable Member States to act in a concerted manner against this form of crime. Ladies and gentlemen! As to dealing with so-called everyday crime, we will continue our course consistently. This includes the actual extension of the out-of-court settlement, which has been successfully implemented for a decade, as well as the speedy implementation of the new criminal procedure to be submitted to Parliament in the spring of 1998. In the future, the diverging provided for in this Directive will allow for a simpler but better coordinated approach to the individual case of minor infringements of less dangerous offenders. This does not mean decriminalisation or privatisation of criminal law. Special attention will be paid to the compensation for damages and also to the other interests of the victims mentioned today in this debate, including an ideal satisfaction. In future, the perpetrator will make a more intense effort under the pressure of the damocles of another threat of condemnation to the victim. In its effects, this reform will give more to the victims of crimes than any judicial law in the Second Republic before. This path will also be pursued with regard to the legal status of the victim, particularly in the preliminary proceedings. However, it is also important to better inform victims of crime about their claims and other possibilities than before. I have been in contact with the solicitor's office in this regard, and it will be possible in Bälde that the judicial system, together with the attorney's office, will provide regular victim advice to the courts. Ladies and gentlemen! Our path of so to speak intelligent hardness on the one hand and sensitive assistance on the other hand is best seen in the addiction law, which has been in force since the beginning of this year, today repeatedly addressed in conjunction with the last criminal law amendment. The concept is: Larger dealers are being removed from the market for a long time, criminal organizations are trying to meet with capital expenditure in their financial center, but we are trying, as far as possible, to help first. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] It is precisely because the ads concern consumers of illegal drugs that it is particularly important not to want to solve the problem of drugs by purely repressive measures, i.e. the use of criminal law, but to be aware that addiction and dependence are primarily medical problems, so that it needs medical and therapeutic approaches beyond criminal law. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ, the Greens and the Liberal Forum]] In particular, with our new legislation on addiction, Austria shows the way to an exemplary balance between criminal and health interventions, and I see no reason to question the solutions taken there a few days after entry into force. Harmonisation of different approaches to the drug problem in the field of prevention, therapy and law enforcement, which has been highly successful in the Austrian Drugs Act, will also enable us, within the framework of the Austrian EU Presidency, to show a practical way for dealing with drug problems across Europe. Ladies and gentlemen! The statistics published here first allow this presumption. It is possible to complete the tenth and eleventh years of schooling if it has been established by the responsible body that the training is targeted and that the child concerned can make sure that he completes the primary schooling. In this case I am fully on your side that we offer appropriate support groups so that this possibility exists. I know a number of schools across Austria, which already offer such support groups to reclaim the primary school for children with special educational needs. However, this cannot be offered to him with integration into secondary level II, but with a special offer in a special support group. And that's what I'm going to do!

ParlaMint-AT_1998-01-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00107_d7e18011	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! In view of the sometimes heated debate on the new spelling, the following has been recalled: The current reform work builds on the work from 1901. In 1901 and in the years that followed, many wise culturally critical minds spoke out against the then spelling reform, for example Thomas Mann, Karl Kraus wrote the "elegie to a lost sound", namely to the mute H. He regretted that the Thau had lost the Heavenly One, because he is now equated with the ship's stow. The history of criticism continued. Renewal – be they even more minimal, homogeneous or gentle – has always been the subject of resistance. In 1954 one protested against the 1901 rule. Mrs Federal Minister, ladies and gentlemen: We should also be aware of this! I believe that the way in which the current reform is being implemented is a good one. This can be seen from the first surveys on satisfaction in the public schools. I just call you a few numbers: 99 percent of adult students are already informed according to the new spelling; 93 percent indicate that the changeover does not pose significant problems, and in nearly 70 percent of public schools, there were no complaints from the parents either. 72 percent consider the methodological didactic reliefs associated with this reform to be useful. [[Abg. Dr. Graf: Who has been questioned: the teachers?]] Teachers and parents! [[Abg. Dr. Graf: Aha!]] Finally, there is an important reference, especially to those who speak of excessive costs and expenses that this reform is supposed to cause, which, if the S-writing is abandoned, account for 1% of the vocabulary. It is gratifying that the school book publishers have managed this change and passed on the changeover costs not to parents and to the market, but to rationalizations. It is also gratifying that children's and youth book authors are positive about this, whose books are already printed after the new spelling or are essentially already in conversion. It is important that teachers have participated in the educational institutions during their continuing training in the framework of the ordinary budgets, the regular teaching of further education courses and further participation. – In other words, no one can be accused of excessive stress. I believe that this reform work is clearer and more uniform than the previous one, and that it makes the rules generally more understandable. The focus is on the systematic processing and recasting of the rules, without abandoning tradition and writing culture. Another last comment on the situation in Germany: There is, as has already been said in another context, another legal culture there. If an amendment is declared essential there, it must be enshrined as a law under the principle of substance. In Austria, there is another legal culture, and for good reasons I see no need for a legal anchoring of the reform. We therefore agree to the spelling reform, but we reject this request.

In this I see a massive restriction of our own sovereignty! This system of the Convention on Legal Assistance, which is almost out of place here in the Order of the Public, is not suitable for fulfilling the intentions and serving the interests of Austria. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Too different, ladies and gentlemen, are the two legal systems in the area of criminal law of the United States of America and Austria on the one hand. In the US, criminal law enforcement is a country matter, and there is no even a codified criminal law because there is no known codification of the legal matter in Anglo-American law. In the United States, the case-law system applies, and it is not just a legal system, but 52 legal systems of the individual states. And that is inconceivable for Austria, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] On the basis of a daily theme, this argument can also be proved by me, namely in the case of the confiscation of images of the Leopold Collection by a New York prosecutor. Ladies and gentlemen! This act of confiscation is nothing other than the path of a claimant in the Civil Code, but in order not to be subject to damage to the collection or foundation Leopold. In this case, a public prosecutor has been used who, by way of detour, tries to raise civil claims and exert pressure on the Republic of Austria and the Leopold Foundation. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! It is clear from the example of this case that this Convention on Legal Assistance is too large, because the sovereignty of the Republic of Austria and the Order of the Public are not preserved because the Republic of Austria would also be obliged, in a case where images of the Leopold Collection are taken under this State Treaty, to carry out surveys here in Austria, although there is no clear criminal action but a good faith. In the European legal area, a acquisition of credulity is clearly defined in accordance with § 367 ABGB and § 366 HGB. And this also shows the complete inequality of treatment: In the United States, the ownership chain must be traced back to the original original owner. Ladies and gentlemen! I support the Convention on Legal Assistance, but they must not go so far as to give up the sovereignty of Austria almost completely.

And with this, Mr Krüger, the legal situation of the European Convention on Legal Assistance has been restored to and for itself. In addition, I believe that I can understand this Treaty in such a way that, even if this Treaty is ratified, legal assistance is not excluded on the basis of reciprocity in accordance with § 3 of the Extradition and Legal Assistance Act, so that we take careful account of the relevant objections. This cannot therefore be requested under this Treaty. [[Intrusion of the Abg. May. Stadler.]] I hope the Minister doesn't see it any differently! ParlaMint-AT_1998-01-22-020-XX-NRSITZ-00107_d7e18648	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! First of all, a fundamental statement: The Treaty we are now discussing is a legal aid agreement. A completely different instrument is the extradition contract. However, this Treaty has nothing to do with extradition. This is about other legal aid measures. It is true that, in principle, we have refrained from both sides of the world. This is a development, as it is now generally beginning. There is, however, a great restriction – and therefore the Ordre public is not injured –: For all those cases where voluntary cooperation with us does not have to be applied as a result of the request and the measures we have taken, for those cases in which Austria has to carry out forced measures or similar measures, we have reserved each other with mutual criminality. For example: If we should hear a witness, we will invite the witness. If he wants to make the testimony here and not in America, then he can be agreed with us. But we can't force him to do that. Stadler: Is it to be ruled out that we must participate in a criminal proceedings in which death penalty can be imposed? That's the problem!]] Of course, we must also make extradition if there is a death penalty on a crime there. However, this Treaty assures us that the death penalty will not be imposed or executed. In addition, I do not believe that there is a crime in the United States which is threatened with death and is not punishable at all in Austria. [[Further calls to the liberals.]] Mr Krüger! I believe that the concerns you have raised are unfounded, because the Order Public will not be hurt. We are only giving legal aid in those cases in which we do not take decisive measures. In the latter case, mutual criminality would be necessary.

This is now the third legislative period, which I am a Member of this House, and in each of these legislative periods there were at least first readings on the revision of citizenship law. Furthermore, Mr Klubobmann Khol, we have not yet come, although this discussion has already begun in the 1980s. [[Abg. Dr. Khol: Just wait! We will fully satisfy you!]] [[Abg. Dr. Khol: Much earlier! Even in the last century! At the time of Starzynski!]] At the end of the 1980s, there were proposals which – and this is not a coincidence – were very similar to those of the Greens. At least our proposals or our own initiative proposal are very similar to the proposals of the Ministry of the Interior. Let me, at the end of my remarks, just give you an overview of those points of the proposal for an initiative that I consider to be the most in need of innovation or that I need most of the applicable Austrian citizenship law. In my opinion, the most important thing is to facilitate citizenship for generations of follow-up. The law in force does not provide for people born in Austria to automatically acquire Austrian citizenship at birth if their parents – and that is the essential! – were already born in Austria. That's what science calls the so-called double ius solo. Children born in Austria, although their parents were already born in Austria, do not automatically become citizens in Austria. These children are not – let me say this now – automatically Austrians and Austrians, although they are actually. No one can explain to me that children whose parents have already been born in Austria are not Austrians, both from the feeling and from the way others see this matter. That's the first point. The second point is not insignificantly important, namely the demand for a complete equality between married and unmarried children in citizenship law. There are gaps and shortcomings. The third point is probably the most controversial, namely the possibility of double citizenship. The fourth and fifth points refer to the deadlines. It is applicable right in Austria to grant the right to citizenship only after 30 years of residence in the federal territory – residence in the federal territory is the main residence in the federal territory. 30 years! This period is unique in European comparison, which is uniquely long. We Greens propose shortening these to ten years and allowing the award of citizenship after five years. At the same time, we also call for the limitation of discretionary powers provided by the law today to give greater legal certainty to the citizens of citizenship or to the neo-citizens. Another point is the possibility of appeal for decisions to the respective UVs. This possibility seems necessary in the sense of greater legal certainty and also the possibility of objecting or taking counter-decisions. A very last point – I would almost say a hobby that Mr. Federal Minister has been serving Dr. Pahr for many years – is the elimination of the immensely high fees for citizenship awards. There are few cases, ladies and gentlemen, where foreign families with two children who have long wanted to live in Austria and have Austrian citizenship, i.e. four-member families, costs of 100 000 S and more can arise. This seems to be certainly disproportionately high. I am not in favour of obtaining citizenship free of charge. In Austria, nothing is for free in the sense of free. You have to stick to stamp marks everywhere, and perhaps it is also important for the self-understanding of neo-Austrians to stick to stamp marks. But as the legal situation is present, it is unsatisfying and in need of innovation. [[President Dr. Fischer gives the bell sign]] More about this in the Committee on the Interior and in the event of any change.
